國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和訊號 
經濟 
⚫ S&P Global 公布，9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數(PMI Composite Output 
Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4，創逾 5 年新高；服務業及製造業
PMI 分別升至 58.7 及 57.0。在內需強勁帶動下，新訂單與就業同步成
長，惟供應鏈瓶頸及能源成本攀升推升通膨壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國新屋銷售年化率為 68.4 萬戶，月增 6.4%、年減 2.0%，顯示銷
售較前月回升但需求尚未完全恢復。中西部與南部為主要支撐，惟房價
年減、庫存仍維持 48 萬戶以上，供給相對充裕，後續銷售能否延續仍
待觀察。 
⚫ 9 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.1，月減 7.0%、年減 12.7%，創近四個
月低點。當前狀況與預期指數同步下滑，反映高物價、燃料價格與貿易
不確定性持續壓抑信心；同時通膨預期升溫，後續消費動能仍待觀察。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 中國國家主席習近平於 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪美並與川普舉行峰會 ， 雙方
同意延長貿易休兵至 2027 年 1 月 10 日，推動部分商品降稅、建立 AI
對話機制及持續磋商稀土供應議題，並延續高層溝通。台灣議題未列入
成果清單，關稅、科技競爭及供應鏈等核心分歧仍未化解，後續談判發
展仍待觀察。 
短評 
⚫ 美國 9 月 PMI 強勁擴張，顯示企業活動與就業動能仍具韌性，但投入
成本與售價上升也反映通膨壓力升溫。相較之下，房市需求尚未全面恢
復，消費信心則明顯轉弱，顯示內需前景仍有隱憂；美中延長貿易休兵
雖有助穩定市場，但核心分歧未解，後續不確定性仍高。 
歐 
元 
區 
9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，服務業 PMI 增1.4 點至 53，製造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7。 
短評 
⚫ 儘管 9 月經濟表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲
央行的鷹派態度，使得 10 月升息一碼的可能性些微增加，亦強化未來
升息預期。

9 月綜合 PMI 維持擴張，製造業表現仍具韌性

中 
國 
美中貿易延長休戰，惟後續協商成效仍待觀察 
經濟 ⚫ 1-8 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.6%、17.6%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 習近平赴華府與美國總統川普舉行峰會，雙方同意貿易休戰期延長至
2027 年 1 月 10 日，並成立雙邊貿易理事會，但在科技管制、稀土供應
及關稅等核心議題上，仍未取得突破。 
⚫ 美中雙方就「300 億美元對 300 億美元」對等降稅架構達成共識，包
含中國製造的小型家電、玩具、節日裝飾品及兒童汽車安全座椅等，以
及美國生產的農產品、海鮮、原木、化妝品及醫療器材等。超過90%的
清單內產品將免除雙方此前相互加徵的關稅，並適用最惠國關稅待遇。 
短評 
⚫ 美中雙方貿易休戰期只延長兩個月，反映美國仍保留談判槓桿，持續給
予中國稀土供應履約的壓力。 
⚫ 美中每年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程
設備、電動車、軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，本次降稅實質效益有限。 
東 
(南) 
亞 
亞銀上修亞洲及太平洋經濟體 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.7% 
經濟 
⚫ 亞銀預估 2026 年印度 GDP 為 7.0%，通膨率為 5.0%。 
⚫ 亞銀預估 2026 年東協國家 GDP 為 4.7%，其中越南 GDP 達 7.8%表現
最佳。 
重要 
事件 ⚫ 印尼央行 9 月會議第三度維持基準利率於 5.75%不變。 
短評 ⚫ AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨與信用風險的重要變數。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.9.29 
一、 美國—PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和
訊號 
標普全球 (S&P Global) 公布， 2026 年 9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數 (PMI 
Composite Output Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4， 連續第四個月攀升 ， 創2021
年 7 月以來新高；其中服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)由 56.5 升至 58.7，創
59 個月高點，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 由 53.9 升至 57.0，創
2022 年 5 月以來最高水準，顯示美國企業活動持續強勁擴張。受國內市場需求穩
健帶動，製造業與服務業新訂單同步加速成長，推升整體產出增速升至逾 5 年來
最快水準，企業亦積極擴增人力，就業成長速度創 2022 年 6 月以來新高。另一
方面，積壓訂單增加及供應鏈延遲加劇，反映產能利用率持續攀升；加以近期油
價上漲推升燃料及運輸成本，使企業投入成本增幅升至 2022 年 10 月以來最高，
帶動售價漲幅回升，顯示通膨壓力有所升溫。儘管如此，企業對未來一年營運前
景仍維持正向看法，反映市場對美國經濟韌性及需求持續擴張仍具信心。 
2026 年 8 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整年化率為 68.4 萬戶 ， 較上月增加6.4%，
但較去年同期下降 2.0%，顯示新屋銷售較前月回升，惟仍低於去年同期水準。地
區別表現分歧，其中中西部及南部分別較上月增加 84.9%及 6.9%，東北部及西部
則分別減少 36.1%及 15.2%。價格方面，8 月新屋銷售中位價為 39.37 萬美元，較
上月小升 0.4%，但較去年同期下降 5.8%。供給方面，8 月底經季節性調整後待售
新屋數量為 48.3 萬戶，與上月持平，較去年同期減少 2.0%；按目前銷售速度估
算，供給月數為 8.5 個月，低於上月的 9.0 個月，與去年同期大致持平。整體而
言，8 月美國新屋銷售較前月回升，主要受中西部與南部銷售增加帶動，惟仍低
於去年同期，顯示需求動能尚未完全恢復。價格方面，中位價較去年同期下滑，
反映市場價格仍有調整壓力；同時待售新屋維持在 48 萬戶以上，供給月數仍達
8.5 個月，顯示整體供給相對充裕，後續仍須觀察銷售回升是否具持續性。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 9 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.1，低於上月
的 51.7， 月減7.0%、 年減12.7%， 為近四個月低點 ， 並較2026 年 1 月下降約 15%，
顯示消費者信心持續轉弱。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由51.9 降至 50.9，月減

1.9%、 年減15.7%； 消費者預期指數則由51.5 降至 46.3， 月減10.1%、 年減10.4%，
反映民眾對未來景氣與個人財務前景的看法明顯惡化。調查指出，消費者對高物
價的擔憂持續升高，當前與未來一年個人財務看法均較上月轉弱約一成；短期企
業展望也因燃料價格偏高與貿易爭端再度升溫而明顯下滑。政治立場方面，各陣
營受訪者普遍認為年初以來經濟前景轉弱，其中共和黨人信心較 1 月下降 20%，
民主黨人下降 13%。 通膨預期方面 ， 未來一年通膨預期由8 月的 4.0%升至 4.6%，
為今年 6 月以來最高，且高於今年 2 月的 3.4%(美伊爆發衝突之前)及 2024 年各
月水準；長期通膨預期亦由 3.3%升至 3.4%，結束連續三個月持平，並高於 2024
年 2.8%至 3.2%的區間。整體而言，9 月美國消費信心再度降溫，顯示高物價、燃
料價格與貿易不確定性持續壓抑民眾信心，後續仍須觀察通膨預期升溫是否進一
步影響消費動能。 
中國國家主席習近平於 2026 年 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪問美國，並於 9 月 24 日
在華府白宮與美國總統川普舉行峰會。會後，美中雙方未發布聯合聲明，而是分
別公布成果清單，顯示雙方在穩定雙邊關係及維持高層溝通方面取得部分共識，
包括同意建構「基於尊重、公平、對等的建設性戰略穩定關係」、推動 300 億美
元非敏感商品對等降稅安排、建立人工智慧 (AI)或「超級智慧」對話與風險溝通
機制，以及持續就稀土與關鍵礦產供應鏈議題進行磋商。此外，美方並表示中國
將於 2027 年及 2028 年分別進口至少 1,000 萬公噸美國煤炭。值得注意的是，台
灣議題並未出現在雙方公布的成果文件中；另一方面，中方則額外強調兩軍危機
溝通機制及合作搜尋失蹤美軍遺骸等事項。整體而言，本次川習會有助延續美中
對話與降低短期緊張情勢，惟雙方在關稅、科技競爭、供應鏈安全及其他戰略議
題上的結構性分歧仍未完全化解，後續談判進展及雙邊互動方向仍有待持續觀察。 
二、歐元區—9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，且為連續四個月上
升。其中服務業PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，創近 10 個月高點，主要反映 AI 與國防投資
活動增加，帶動商業服務需求，抵銷能源價格上漲對消費購買力的負面影響。製
造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7，不過，德國萊茵河水位下降，沿岸較為發達的化工、製
藥業物流成本增加，因而暫時壓抑製造業上行空間。值得注意的是，由於國際原
油價格回升，綜合投入、產出物價指數皆上升至近 4 個月最高。故儘管 9 月經濟
表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲央行的鷹派態度，使得

三、日本—9 月綜合 PMI 維持擴張，製造業表現仍具韌性 
 日本 9 月綜合 PMI 由 53.5 降至 52.5，製造業與服務業同步回落，經濟成長動
能較前兩個月的高檔有所降溫。製造業 PMI 下跌 0.8 點至 54.1，雖然產出、新訂
單及出口訂單增速放緩，但外部需求仍相當強勁，尤其 AI 相關需求可能持續帶
動出口表現，加上企業開始重建庫存、供應鏈瓶頸尚未完全解除，預料補庫存需
求將在未來數月繼續支撐生產成長。服務業 PMI 下降 0.9 點至 51.6，受到工資、
運輸及能源成本上升影響 ， 需求動能略有降溫 ， 但積壓訂單與出口業務有所改善 。
另，投入成本與售價漲幅均較前期放緩，顯示通膨壓力正逐步緩解，但整體物價
水準仍維持高檔。此外，企業對未來景氣的信心持續改善，未來活動指數升至七
個月高點，反映企業普遍預期經濟擴張趨勢仍可望延續。 
四、中國—美中貿易延長休戰，惟後續協商成效仍待觀察 
1-8 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.6%、17.6%，其中營業收入年
增率較上期(1-7 月)略增 0.1 個百分點，但利潤年增率下降 1.9 個百分點。受惠於
金屬、塑化原物料上漲，加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因
素，營收成長貢獻度較高者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 31.1%、71.8%)、
計算機、通信和其他電子設備製造業(營收、利潤年增20.1%、109.9%)、化學原料
及製品(營收、利潤年增 9.1%、51%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年
增率雖分別為 11.5%、2.9%，但獲利分別年減 5.2%、16%。非金屬礦物製品業營
收、獲利下滑 9.5、46.7%，黑色金屬加工業營收、獲利分別年減 1.1%、62.4%，
反映產業持續進行減產所致。整體而言，部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，
以及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較佳，惟部分產業因陷入產能調整週期，或
政府減少支持等因素而表現較弱，產業分化明顯。 
展望未來，儘管美中雙方互相降低等值 300 億美元產品關稅，惟考量雙方每
年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程設備、電動車、
軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，因此實質效益有限。另外，雖然美中雙方同意延長貿
易休戰，但休戰期只延長兩個月，反映美國仍保留談判槓桿，持續給予中國稀土
供應履約的壓力。後續將關注美中能否在休戰期間就開放市場、科技競爭、供應

鏈安全等議題取得進一步共識，若談判不如預期，美國仍可能重新祭出新的反制
措施 ， 而中國亦可能運用稀土作為談判籌碼 ， 經貿關係不確定性仍是較高 。 此外 ，
預計 10 月 8 日舉行歐中貿易會談，若談判未能取得突破，歐盟對中國的貿易限
制恐進一步升級，對中國出口形成額外壓力。 
五、印度及東南亞—亞銀上修亞洲及太平洋經濟體 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.7% 
亞 洲 開 發 銀 行 (ADB) 最 新 經 濟 展 望 (Asian Development Outlook, ADO 
September 2026 )上修 2026 年亞洲及太平洋地區經濟體 2026 年經濟成長率(GDP)
至 4.7%(7 月預估 4.5%)、2027 年為 4.5%。ADB 認為，亞洲開發中經濟體雖面臨
中東衝突延長、俄烏戰爭再升溫、能源價格高檔震盪及強烈聖嬰現象等逆風，但
在政府刺激政策、民間投資及 AI 帶動科技出口支撐下，區域經濟仍展現韌性。
通膨方面，2026 年通膨率下修至 3.9%，惟能源與糧食價格壓力仍高，2027 年通
膨預測上修至 3.3%。 
區域經濟表現方面 ， 南亞成長最強勁 ，2026 年 GDP 成長率預估上修至 6.4%，
主要受印度內需與投資帶動；東亞則受中國內需疲弱影響，成長維持平穩；東協
國家(ASEAN)上調至 4.7%， 其中越南 、 馬來西亞及泰國受惠於科技與製造業投資
而獲上修。中亞及西亞、太平洋地區則面臨能源及食品價格上漲壓力，成長預測
略為下修至 3.7%、3.0%。(詳下表) 
ADB 認為未來最大風險包括地緣政治衝突升級、聖嬰 現象造成糧食供應衝
擊，以及 AI 相關資產可能出現價格重估。整體而言，AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞
洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨
與信用風險的重要變數。

五、台灣—AI 浪潮推升接單動能，外銷訂單再創新高 
8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6 億美元，首度突破千億美元大關，其中受惠於 ASIC
晶片需求帶動，資通產品接單翻倍、電子產品接單亦大增 83.9%，惟光學器材因
面板需求維持低檔，年減 5%。非科技產品方面，基本金屬年增 26.8%，係因銅箔
及銅箔基板受惠 AI 相關供應鏈需求及銅價續漲，加以鍍鋅鋼需求回溫；機械年
增 28.3%，係因半導體業者增加資本支出，帶動半導體設備、自動化設備需求延
續；化學品年增 15.4%，主因藥品接單成長，加上產業用化學品需求持續增加；
塑橡膠製品年增 6.7%， 主係塑膠原料受原料上漲而推升產品售價 ， 加上部分塑膠
製品訂單回溫。展望未來，經濟部統計處預估 2026 年外銷訂單有望突破 1 兆美
元，在 AI 相關需求持續擴張帶動下，科技產品表現可望維持強勁，而傳統產業
中下游產品亦可望受惠於相關供應鏈需求，惟上游原物料價格競爭激烈，後續仍
須關注第四季消費旺季是否有相關備貨需求。

3Q largely in-line; mix-driven margin dilution is well understood — Sinbon 
reported 3Q26 EPS of NT$3.79 (+1% q/q and +24% y/y), broadly in line with our 
estimate and 4% below consensus. Revenue rose 8% q/q and +28% y/y to 
NT$9.7bn, topping our/BBG forecast 4%/2%. GPM decreased 77bps to 23.8% vs. 
our estimate of 24.6%, primarily reflecting rising semi cabinet assembly sales and 
increased shipments of auto and communications. We believe the market is well 
aware of this mix-driven dilution. Opex remained at an elevated level due to 
increased product development and sampling, alongside higher freight expenses. 
Operating profits +50% y/y, missing our/BBG estimates 4%/7%, respectively.

5.5bn in 2027E from NT$3–3.5bn in 2026E, led by solid demand from two major 
semi customers. While increasing contribution from this business could dilute group 
GPM, it should support sales and absolute gross profit growth. Emerging

Implications — We believe the margin implications of a rising semi sales mix are well
understood by the market and leave our investment thesis intact. Our constructive
view is based on steady growth in existing businesses, supplemented by medium- to
long-term opportunities in humanoid robots, drones, and LEO satellites. We also

view Sinbon as a relatively defensive stock amid AI-related market volatility, given
its limited direct AI exposure and diversified business portfolio. The shares could
benefit from investor rotation toward companies with resilient underlying
fundamentals, while emerging applications provide additional support for the
longer-term growth outlook.

Sinbon
Valuation
Our TP for Sinbon is NT$370. We value Sinbon on PE to capture the company’s earnings growth prospects. We set our target
PE at 23x, which we set at a 20% premium to the historical average of 19x since 2018. We believe 23x is justified in view of
improving fundamentals with strong momentum of industrials segment, driving a clear margin expansion and solid earnings
growth in 2026E. Applying this to our 2H26-1H27E EPS forecast, we arrive at our target price of NT$370.

Risks
Key downside risks that could cause Sinbon's share to deviate from our target price include 1) slower-than-expected growth in
non-IT/non-consumer applications; 2) Intensified competition; 3) Raw material price hike and labor cost increases; 4) FX
volatility; 5) Greater-than-expected impact from the geopolitical conflict.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned
to a Catalyst Watch Upside, Catalyst Watch Downside, STV Upside, or STV Downside call is considered Catalyst Watch/STV

3Q26 獲利預估數上修因 AI 及車用優於原估計 
3Q26 營收 42 億元，季增/年增 13%/20%，估計 EPS 上修 16%至 2.07 元，
季增 28%且年增 39%；上修主因該季 AI/車用營收優於原估計 9%/20%，營
收占比 16%/29%，年增 76%/44%。4Q26 估計營收 40 億元，年增 20%，
EPS 上修 12%至 1.86 元，年增 41%；上修主因上調車用營收 10%至年增
35%，且 AI 應用估計占比 16%，年增 75%。 
Photo die 產能規劃持續擴充，元件頻率與市場 AI 同步升級 
光收發模組於 800G 世代主要使用  156.25MHz 的產品； 1.6T 世代需要
312.5MHz 的石英元件，搭配黃光微影（Photo die ）製程。以交換機而言，
低於 25.6T 的舊世代交換機主要使用 156.25MHz 的石英元件；51.2T 等級
（主流 AI 交換機）及 102.4T 等級（下世代交換機），升級至 312.5MHz 石
英振盪器。因應持續增加的高速傳輸需求，晶技的 Photo die 產能規劃由目
前的 8KK/月增加至 2027 年中的 24KK/月，規劃 2028 年底達到 40KK。 
2027 年 AI 占比 23%且年增 64%，維持正向看法 
本中心預估 2026 年營收上修 5%且 EPS 上修 7%至 6.86 元，年增 30%，因
AI 及車用上修 3%及 8%。2027 年營收上修 2%至 165 億元，年增 9%，
EPS 上修 5%至 8.44 元，年增 23%；上修主因 AI 上修 6%且車用上修 2%，
手機則下修 2%。本中心正面看待 2027 年 AI 的營收占比 23%且年增 64%，
超越手機占比 19%，且 AI 占比高於 2025/2026 的 10%/15%。參考市場上
光通訊兼具手機零件概念公司本益比 10-50 倍，本中心以 2027 年 EPS 8.44
元及 28 倍本益比(前次以 2027 年 EPS 28 倍)，給予目標價 236 元，維持買
進評等。

元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 EPS 上修 16%至 2.07 元，年增 39%，因營收占比 16%/29%的
AI/車用優於原估計 9%/20%；4Q26 EPS 上修 12%至 1.86 元。 
⧫  
石英元件因 AI 升級 Photo die 需求提升；晶技 Photo die 產能規劃由
目前的 8KK/月增加至 2027 年中的 24KK/月，2028 年底達到 40KK。 
⧫  
本中心估計 2027 年 AI 的營收占比由 22%上修至 23%，2026/2027 年
增率 65%/64%，為主要成長動能，維持正向看法及買進評等。

3Q26 營收 42 億元，季增/年增 13%/20%，估計 EPS 上修 16%至 2.07 元，季增 28%且年增 39%；

### Page 3

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 10 月 27 日 TXC 第3 頁，共 14頁  
石英元件頻率與 AI 需求同步升級，由 156.25MHz 邁向 312.5MHz 
光收發模組於 800G 世代主要使用 156.25MHz 的產品；1.6T 世代，傳輸速度太快，晶片倍頻產生
的雜訊會摧毀訊號。此時必須使用基頻為  312.5MHz 的石英元件，搭配黃光微影（ Photo die ）製
程，將相位抖動壓低到 30 飛秒（fs）等級，才能確保光電轉換過程中資料不失真。。 
以交換機而言，低於 25.6T 的舊世代交換機主要使用 156.25MHz 的石英元件；51.2T 等級（主流 
AI 交換機）及 102.4T 等級（下世代頂級 AI 交換機），升級至 312.5MHz 石英振盪器，以免 Jitter 
過大讓傳輸失真。交換機的插槽 (Port)速度而言，800G 乙太網路（Ethernet）/ InfiniBand 埠部分
高階 800G 交換機已提前採用 312.5MHz 晶片方案；1.6T 乙太網路均需採用 312.5MHz（且相位抖
動 < 30 飛秒 fs） 的石英元件。 
圖 3：MHz 石英振盪器(左)；恆溫控制石英振盪器(右)

資料來源：公司資料、元大投顧整理

Photo die 產能規劃持續擴充，由目前的 8KK 至 2028 年底達 40KK 
Photo die 石英元件以半導體等級的黃光製程曝光、顯影與化學蝕刻來製作石英晶片；相對於傳統
的石英元件利用機械研磨或拋光的方式把石英切割成薄片（ Die）而言，在石英元件要求越小且頻
率要求越來越高時，傳統機械加工會達到物理極限，容易震碎且精準度不夠，故需要 Photo die 製
程將石英晶片做得極度微型化，藉此產生超高頻率與超低訊號抖動。 
晶技的 Photo die 產能規劃由目前的 8KK/月增加至 2027 年中的 24KK/月，規劃 2028 年底達到
40KK，因應高速傳輸需求。 AI 占營收比重估計於 2026/2027 年為 15%/23%(2024/2025 年為
8%/10%)，年增率為 65%/64%，以光收發模組 DSP 搭配的石英元件 Oscillator 為主要動能；此外，
交換機 ASIC 搭配的石英元件以及 AI 加速器應用亦為動能。 
圖 4：Photo Die(光電二極體晶片) 月產能規劃 
Photo Die 月產能 
時間 2026/10 2027F 2028F 
產能(單位:萬) 800 2,400 4,000

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 10 月 27 日 TXC 第5 頁，共 14頁  
產業概況  
石英元件能提供穩定頻率信號，為網通設備及晶片提供精確時脈 
石英元件利用石英晶體的壓電效應，將機械能及電能轉換成基本訊號源，用來產生高精度振盪頻率
的一種電子元件。石英晶體(Crystal Resonators 或 Xtal)是石英晶棒經由切割、研磨等前段製程來
產出石英晶片(Blank)後，石英晶片加上電極與外殼封裝；其提供固定時脈信號，作為控頻及計時之
用；為單純石英晶體被動元件，不含主動元件，需搭配外加電路才會產生振盪。石英晶體振盪器
(crystal oscillator ，簡稱 OSC 或 XO)為內含石英晶體加上設計有振盪 IC 形成信號源模組，需要電
源，可直接產生振盪訊號輸出；整個模組屬主動元件，通常是四支接腳的電子元件。  
石英元件像是節拍器，是讓產品數位訊號能夠對標的關鍵零組件，能夠提供穩定的頻率信號；石英
振盪器，為伺服器、網通設備提供穩定和精確的時脈訊號，確保數據傳輸和處理的同步性和可靠性。  
晶技營收規模及獲利能力相較於台系同業具優勢 
晶技與其他石英元件台系同業相較，營收規模為其他台系業者的 10-20 倍，毛利率及營業利益率亦
優於台系業者。主因晶技早期佈局高階產品，且技術能力持續提升，能供應市場需求持續提供微型
化及高頻化產品。 
圖 6：晶技營收規模高於台灣同業，毛利率及營益率亦有較良好表現

石英元件產業重回成長，其中小型化產品成長動能相對強勁 
根據 CS&A 預估全球石英元件市場產值 2020 因受 COVID-19 影響，產值年衰退 6.9%， 2024 年開
始回到成長軌道；且相較整體市場之緩和成長，小型化產品成長力道更佳。

獲利調整與股票評價
正面看待 AI 貢獻 2026 及 2027 年營運且上修獲利，維持買進建議
本中心預估 2026 年 EPS 上修 7%至 6.86 元，年增 30%；2027 年營收上修 2%至 165 億元，年增
9%，EPS 上修 5%至 8.44 元，年增 23%。上修主因 AI 上修 6%且車用上修 2%，手機則下修 2%。
本中心正面看待 2027 年 AI 的營收占比 23%超越手機應用的 19%，參考市場上光通訊兼具手機零
件概念公司本益比 10-50 倍，本中心以 2027 年 EPS 8.44 元及 28 倍本益比(前次以 2027 年 EPS
28 倍)，給予目標價 236 元。

公司簡介  
全球石英元件領導廠商，具台灣、中國及印尼產能 
台灣晶技成立於 1983 年，主要產品為石英元件，包含石英晶體諧振器 (Crystal Resonator ；XTAL)、石英振盪器 (Crystal 
Oscillators；XO)，應用於電子產品頻率控制。產能主要位於台灣平鎮、中國重慶及寧波、印尼泗水。高階產品的技術朝小
型化、高頻低雜訊、高精度及穩定性發展。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 晶技整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於領先的位置，
在電子零組件行業中的 公司中排名亦領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 晶技的整體曝險屬於較低水準，且優於電子零組件行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的  ESG 
議題包含「碳排放－自身營運」、「商業道德」以及「資源使用」這些實質 ESG 議題上面臨最高的風險敞口。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：晶技在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬中等，公司已採取一些措施來管理
與重大 ESG 議題相關的風險，但這些措施尚不足以涵蓋其所面臨的所有風險。儘管如此，該公司並未捲入任何具有重
大財務影響的風險事件。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

Document 157: 35 岁不会写代码？这波 AI 红利，可能就轮到你了

Source PDF: all report pdf/35 岁不会写代码？这波 AI 红利，可能就轮到你了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)

### Page 1

客⼾现在缺的，是既懂业务怎么运转、⼜能亲⼿把  AI 从⼀个想法做成上线系统的⼈。
摘要：
数据显⽰，全球科技公司年内已宣布裁员近  20 万⼈。与此同时，有⼀个  AI 岗位的招聘量却在
⼀年⾥暴涨了  7 倍多，薪资中位数折合⼈⺠币约  130 万元。

OpenAI 、 Anthropic 、微软、亚⻢逊、⾕歌，⼏家巨头前后砸下近百亿美元，有的开新公司，有
的建新部⻔，抢的都是这类⼈才。最反常识的是：这个岗位最稀缺的本事，恰恰不是写代码。

Anthropic ：培养  1 万个 “ 把  AI 送进企业的⼈ ”

10 ⽉  2 ⽇， Claude 的开发商  Anthropic 宣布投⼊  1 亿美元成⽴ “Claude 前沿学院 ” （ Claude
Frontier Academy ），⽬标是在  2027 年底前培养  1 万名 “ 前沿部署⼯程师 ” 。

⾸批学员是从合作伙伴中选出的在职⼯程师，来⾃埃森哲、摩根⼠丹利、诺和诺德和⼏家顶级
咨询公司。培训分两段：先是  4 天线下集训，从⼀次 “ 模拟企业部署 ” 开始；考核通过后，学员回
到⾃⼰公司，带⼀个真实项⽬做  12 周实战。⾸批认证预计在  2027 年初完成。

在  Anthropic 看来，企业落地  AI 的瓶颈不在模型，⽽在于缺少能推动变⾰的⼈。负责这个项⽬
的⾼管  Steve Corfield 说，客⼾现在缺的，是既懂业务怎么运转、⼜能亲⼿把  AI 从⼀个想法做
成上线系统的⼈。

这只是今年  AI 巨头抢⼈⼤战中的最新⼀例。

砸下百亿美元，巨头们都在抢同⼀种⼈才

今年  5 ⽉， OpenAI 成⽴了专⻔帮企业落地  AI 的 “OpenAI 部署公司 ” ，由  TPG 领投，⾸轮融资
超过  40 亿美元。同⽉， Anthropic 与⿊⽯、 Hellman & Friedman 、⾼盛合资成⽴了⼀家  AI 服
务公司，各⽅初始投资约  15 亿美元。

6 ⽉底到  7 ⽉初，亚⻢逊  AWS 和微软先后出⼿： AWS 投⼊  10 亿美元，组建⾃⼰的部署⼯程
团队；微软投⼊  25 亿美元成⽴新部⻔，把约  6000 名⼯程专家派驻到客⼾企业。 9 ⽉，⾕歌云
和埃森哲成⽴联合业务部⻔，计划培训最多  1000 名埃森哲⼯程师，专⻔派驻客⼾现场。

过去这些公司⽐的是谁的模型更强、参数更⼤、跑分更⾼，如今却不约⽽同地把钱砸向同⼀个
⽅向：派⼈进客⼾公司，把  AI 真正⽤起来。

这类⼈才在业内有个统⼀的叫法：FDE （ Forward Deployed Engineer ），中⽂常译作“ 前线部
署⼯程师 ”。

招聘量三年猛涨 30 倍，年薪中位数超百万元

熟悉美股的读者，对这个词的源头不会陌⽣。 FDE 最早是  Palantir （帕兰泰尔）在⼗多年前系
统化推⾏的⼀种做法：⼯程师不坐在总部写通⽤产品，⽽是常驻客⼾现场，和客⼾⽤同⼀套数
据、扛同⼀个业务指标，把模糊的需求、分散的数据和落不了地的⽬标，⼀点点变成真正能⽤
的系统。

当年很多⼈觉得这种模式太重、太慢，不像⼀家软件公司该⼲的事。今天回头看，这恰恰是
Palantir 最难被复制的护城河： 2026 年第⼆季度， Palantir 营收  19.35 亿美元，同⽐增⻓
93% ，其中美国商业收⼊同⽐增⻓  149% 。公司⾸席执⾏官  Alex Karp 有⼀个在业内被反复引⽤
的判断：⼤模型当然重要，但⾄少在未来⼏年⾥，价值在于落地实施。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

据劳动⼒市场分析机构  Lightcast 统计， 2023 年全美  FDE 招聘⼴告不到  200 条， 2025 年约
1200 条， 2026 年仅前九个⽉就已超过  7300 条。这些⼴告标出的薪资中位数约  19.5 万美元，
⽐  2023 年⾼出  40% ，是全美所有招聘⼴告薪资中位数的三倍多。 Indeed 的数据也指向同⼀个
⽅向：美国  FDE 职位从  2025 年  4 ⽉的  643 个增⾄  2026 年  4 ⽉的  5330 个，⼀年涨了  7 倍
多。

值得注意的是，今年前九个⽉发布  FDE 招聘⼴告最多的雇主中，德勤、埃森哲等咨询公司排在
了⾕歌等科技巨头之前。可⻅  FDE 已不只是  AI 公司的内部技术岗位，任何想把  AI ⽤进业务的
企业都需要这样的⼈。

需要说明的是， FDE 不等于 “ 驻场外包 ” 。外包是甲⽅说什么就做什么；FDE 则要先判断⼀件事
该不该⽤  AI 、⽤哪个模型、花多少钱、怎么证明有效，再把它真正落地上线。它要解决的是企
业⾥的⼀个⽼难题：懂技术的不懂业务，懂业务的不懂技术。

AI 落地难在业务，稀缺的恰恰是懂业务的⼈

很多⼈看到这⾥会想：这是年轻程序员的机会，我不写代码，也过了转⾏的年纪。

但不妨看看  Anthropic 怎么描述⼀个好的  FDE ：熟悉  Agent 系统、擅⻓解决问题、善于沟通、
有⼯程经验。这四条⾥，有⼀半在技术之外。

过去两年，⼏乎每家企业都买过  AI 产品、做过  AI 试点，但真正跑进⽇常业务的远⽐宣传的
少。⼏组被业内⼴泛引⽤的数据或许能说明⼀些问题：标普全球的调查显⽰，企业  AI 项⽬的弃
⽤率⼀年内从  17% 升到了  42%； Gartner 预计，到  2027 年超过四成的  Agent 项⽬会被取消；
IDC 与联想  2025 年的⼀份报告则显⽰，88% 的  AI 概念验证项⽬最终没有投⼊⽣产。

问题往往不出在模型上 —— 演⽰时⼀切顺利，⼀进⼊真实业务，就会碰上脏数据、⽼系统、权
限审批、员⼯习惯，以及 “ 出了错谁负责 ” 。毕⻢威的⼀位⾸席技术架构师举过⼀个例⼦：⼀个应
⽤先花了⼏个⽉梳理需求和业务背景，真正⽤  AI 开发只⽤了⼤约⼀个⽉。

FDE 真正值钱的那⼀半能⼒不是编程，⽽是懂业务。⼀份合同哪⼏条要紧，⼀张报表哪个数字
会出事，⼀个审批流程卡在哪⼀步，哪件事交给机器做会让⽼板睡不着觉，这些  AI 替代不了，
反⽽是在⾏业⾥⼲了⼗⼏、⼆⼗年的⼈最清楚。

你缺少的不是经验，⽽是把经验交给  AI 去执⾏的那⼀步：知道今天的  AI 能做什么、不能做什
么、该怎么搭、要花多少钱、出了错怎么兜底。

我们这次的训练营，就是为这⼀步准备的。

两天⾛完  AI 落地最后⼀公⾥

华尔街⻅闻⾸期「 FDE 实战训练营」将于  11 ⽉  14 ⽇⾄  15 ⽇在上海开班。这是⼀个线下实战
营，限量  50 席，在两天时间⾥，带你从零做出⼀个真正能⽤、能上线的  AI Agent 。

先说说  Agent （智能体）是什么。很多⼈对  AI 的印象还停留在 “ 问⼀句答⼀句 ” 的聊天上，但
Agent 不⼀样：你交给它⼀件事，它会⾃⼰查资料、打开软件、读写⽂件、交出结果，做错了还
能⾃⼰返⼯。巨头们派  FDE 进企业，部署的正是这种技术。

两天训练营共  12 个模块，从模型怎么选、价格怎么算，⼀直讲到评测、成本优化和安全上线，
都是⼤⼚把  AI 做上线时真正在⽤的⽅法。主讲讲师  Right 曾任腾讯⾼级算法⼯程师，主导过微
信核⼼  AI Agent 项⽬的算法设计和规模化落地，在  Agent 系统设计、模型训练、 Harness 架构
⽅向有深厚积累。他还在  NeurIPS 、 ACL 等顶级会议发表过多篇  Agent ⽅向论⽂，相关⼯作被
Google DeepMind 引⽤。

力成為全球第五大封測廠， 以發展記憶體積體電路之封測業務為策略， 原以記憶體
測試業務起家 ，2000 年購入力晶後段設備後開始提供客戶封裝加測試一貫化服務 ， 
2012 年入股超豐電子(2441 TT) 後成為其大股東，並提供邏輯 IC 封裝業務。目前
持有超豐股權約 43%。主要客戶為鎧俠、SK Hynix(Solidigm)、美光、金士頓、南
亞科等。3Q26 業務比重為封裝 67%、測試 24%、SiP/Module 9%；產品比重為
DRAM 20%、Flash 28%、SiP/Module 9%、Logic 43%。AI 相關營收目前主要
來自 LPDDR、eSSD 等相關產品。 
3Q26 財報略優預期：力成 3Q26 EPS 3.55 元，略優預期的 3.48 元，毛利率優於
預期，主因產品組合轉佳、封測報價調漲，目前公司封測UTR 維持滿載，且4Q26
仍有調漲報價空間。 
4Q26 營收毛利率維持雙增：公司展望 4Q26 營收續增，三大產品線都將繼續調漲
報價，毛利率因為封測報價繼續調漲將提升，整體展望方向符合預期，獲利預估因
毛 利 率 優 於 預 期 而 上 修 ， 預 估4Q26 營 收 為275.54 億元 (+8.8%QoQ ，
+28.7%YoY)，毛利率 25.7% ，營業利益率 19.6% ， 稅 後 淨 利35.56 億元
(+36.2%QoQ，+90.8%YoY)，稅後 EPS 4.81 元。

FoPLP 進度維持，2027 年邏輯產品線為主要動能：公司主要營收來自 DRAM 和
NAND 封測，並耕耘高階 FCBGA 封裝、FoPLP 等技術已久，2027 年將逐步開花
結果 ， 目前公司在大尺寸晶片封裝已有拿到相關訂單 ， 客戶涵蓋AI 應用晶片業者 、
CSP 等，FoPLP 進度維持 1Q27~2Q27 放量，同樣屬邏輯產品線。公司對 2027
年記憶體市場仍持樂觀態度，表示韓廠產能沒開這麼快， 中國業者的產能則優先供
需中國本土需求仍不夠用，故供需狀況仍看不到反轉跡象。整體研究部認為 2027
年公司動能以邏輯產品為主，記憶體成長幅度相對溫和，預估 2026 年營收為
973.19 億元(+29.9%YoY)，毛利率 23.0%，營業利益率 16.6%，稅後淨利 102.31
億元 (+84.8%YoY)，稅後 EPS 13.86 元， 2027 年 營 收 為1,222.37 億元
(+25.6%YoY)，毛利率 26.3%，營業利益率 20.3%，稅後淨利 167.11 億元
(+63.3%YoY)， 稅後EPS 22.62 元 ， 維持買進建議 ， 目標價340 元(15X 2027 EPS)。

Document 182: 6467泰合 永豐

Source PDF: all report pdf/6467泰合 永豐.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-20

### Page 1

個股聚焦  
2026/10/20

陳奕均 fion.chen@sinopac.com

泰合 (6467 TT) 
開花結果在即

永豐觀點 
抗血栓口溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議買進：泰合 10/20 上市掛牌，掛牌價105.6 元。由於(1) 泰合開發之口溶
膜劑型藥物有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點下，市場競爭力足；(2) 抗血栓口
溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即，2027 年在授權與上市權利金開始認列下 ，營運轉
虧為盈且呈大跳躍狀；(3) 由於多數學名藥廠受限專利訴訟，最快需至 2028 年 4
月後才可上市銷售，促使泰合享有約一年的「黃金市場空窗期」 ，能與原廠直接瓜
分市場。且因 BEQIA 屬 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥，即便2Q28 後學名藥陸續進場，其
獨特的劑型優勢仍能築起價格防護牆，所面臨的價折壓力相對輕微，建議買進，目
標價 170 元( 18 X 2027 EPS )。

營運現況與分析 
公司簡介：泰合生技成立於 2010 年，專注開發 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥與特殊學名
藥，核心技術為自主研發的 TDS 透皮／透黏膜傳輸平台，將現有口服藥品成分改
造為透皮與透黏膜藥物傳導劑型 ，提供方便使用且更安全的新劑型藥物。 產品開發
上，505(b)(2)部分，治療抗血栓口溶膜貼片(每日兩片)BEQIA 靜待歐美取證中外，
其餘則仍在臨床階段中，困難學名藥部分，化療止吐口溶膜2018 年日本取證銷售
中外，其餘四項藥品則在進行洽談授權合作。 
圖一：泰合產品線開發時程

資料來源：泰合；永豐投顧研究部整理，Oct. 2026

### Page 2

2

口溶膜劑型藥物能有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點 ：傳統口服錠劑藥物必須經
過食道、進入胃腸道消化，再經由肝臟代謝後才進入血液循環，泰合開發之口溶膜
劑型藥物， 可讓藥膜在接觸舌頭或口腔黏膜數秒內快速溶解 ， 病患完全不需飲水即
可完成給藥， 且部份藥物分子能直接透過口腔黏膜上皮細胞微血管吸收， 直接進入
全身血液循環， 不僅能提高藥物生體可用率， 還能減少胃酸對藥物的破壞與對胃腸
道的刺激下，有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點，提高患者服藥順從性。 
抗血栓口溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即，提前卡位下，營運動能可期：泰合自主
開發的抗血栓口溶膜新劑型新藥 BEQIA， 預計4Q26 取得歐盟藥證 、2Q27 獲美國
FDA 核准上市。目前該藥原廠在美國市場年銷售額>200 億美元，相較原廠口服錠
劑，泰合 BEQIA 具備三大競爭優勢：不需飲水（服藥便利）、解決吞嚥困難（減
輕照顧端壓力），以及攜帶與給藥便利（優化長照與醫療機構給藥流程）。 
市場競爭方面，由於多數學名藥廠受限專利訴訟，最快需至2028 年 4 月後才可上
市銷售，促使泰合享有約一年的「黃金市場空窗期」 ，能與原廠直接瓜分市場。此
外，由於BEQIA 屬 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥，即便2Q28 後學名藥陸續進場，其獨特
的劑型優勢仍能築起價格防護牆，所面臨的折價壓力應相對輕微。 
泰合海外市場營銷預計將採授權分潤模式，研究部預估 1H27 歐美地區簽約金應
有機會各達 500~1000 萬美金外，隨美國地區 2Q27 上市、通路鋪設及醫保陸續
准入，Peak sales 市佔率 2-3%(預期將落於 2029-30 年)、銷售分潤 12-15%估算
下，稅後 EPS 貢獻將落於 10-20 元。 
財務預估：預估泰合 2026 年營收 0.1 億元，稅後虧損 1.89 億元，稅後 EPS 以掛
牌後股本 5.99 億元估算，稅後 EPS-3.15 元，2027 年預估歐美地區授權簽約金各
500 萬美金及 1000 萬美金 ， 及上市銷售後權利金認列下 ，預估營收將跳升至7.75
億元，稅後淨利 5.68 億元，稅後 EPS 9.49 元。

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
악조건 속에서도 이 정도 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 영업실적은 매출액 5조 7,536억원(yoy +30.2%, qoq 
`-9.1%), 영업이익 8,992억원(yoy +61.3%, qoq -13.5%)으로 당사 추정치에 대체로 
부합하였다. 조업일수 감소와 원화 강세로 매출액이 감소했으며, 임단협 타결로 
인한 성과금 및 격려금이 당분기에 비용으로 반영되었다. 해양플랜트 부문에서는 
RUYA 프로젝트의 공사 진행률 확대와 TRION 프로젝트의 C/O(계약 변경) 
180억원이 추가로 반영되며 매출액이 전분기대비 0.7% 증가하였다. 엔진기계 
부문은 환율 하락을 판가 상승과 D/F(이중연료) 비중 증가로 상쇄하며 전분기와 
유사한 영업실적을 달성했다. 
2027년에도 지속될 수주 시점 효과 
3분기 대형선 야드의 수주 시점별 매출 비중은 2022년 4%, 2023년 45%, 2024년 
31%, 2025년 20% 수준으로 파악된다. 상대적으로 선가가 낮았던 2022~2023년 
물량이 여전히 매출의 절반 가량을 차지하고 있는 셈이다. 따라서 2027년 말까지도 
고선가 물량의 매출 비중 확대가 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이는 LNG운반선 매출 
비중 감소에도 불구하고 수익성을 방어할 수 있는 핵심 요인으로 작용할 전망이다.   
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 550,000원으로 하향 
투자의견은 매수로 유지하나, 목표주가는 550,000원으로 34.5% 하향한다. 환율 
가정을 기존 1,450원 수준에서 1,400원으로 조정함에 따라 목표주가 산정의 기준이 
되는 2027~2028년 실적추정치를 하향조정하였다. 또한 미국 네오 클라우드 
업체들의 자금 조달 여건 악화로 데이터센터 증설이 지연되고 있어, 발전용 엔진 
증설을 근거로 동사에 부여하였던 프리미엄을 제거하여 P/E 배수를 19.8배로 
하향조정했다. 그러나 본업인 상선 부문은 향후 고부가가치 가스선의 발주 강세를 
전망하기 때문에 투자의견은 매수로 유지한다.

盘⾯上，个股呈现普跌态势，沪深京三市超 4700 股飘绿，今⽇成交 1.57 万亿。沪深两市成

板、存储等⼤幅调整，天孚通信触及跌停。银⾏、煤炭、油⽓、电⼒、⽩酒等红利板块全

摘要：
A 股算⼒硬件陷⼊⼤幅调整，光模块、光芯⽚、光通信等概念股集体下挫，此前美股⾕歌因业绩
令⼈失望⼤跌 10%，且亚太市场三星电⼦跌超 4% ，第三季度芯⽚利润不及预期。 SK 海⼒⼠盘中
⼀度转跌，此前⼀度涨近 7% 。
10 ⽉ 29 ⽇， A 股全天低开低⾛，三⼤股指集体下跌，临近尾盘，创业板跌幅进⼀步扩⼤，收盘跌
超 4% ，科创 50 指数亦跌 4% ，算⼒硬件（ CPO/ 光模块 /PCB ）与半导体、存储、⼈形机器⼈等前
期强势⽅向集体重挫，光模块⻰头股 “ 易中天 ” ⼤跌，天孚通信逼近跌停。银⾏逆势⾛强，⼯商银
⾏、建设银⾏双双再创新⾼，四⼤⾏总市值突破 10 万亿元⼤关。券商板块亦承压，中⾦公司午

后跌停，华鑫股份、财达证券、西部证券等跌幅居前。消息⾯上， 10 ⽉ 29 ⽇，中⾦公司 11.36 亿
股股份迎来解禁。值得注意的是，多只沪深 300ETF 集中放量。

块等科技股⼤幅⾛低， AI ⼤模型股低开低⾛，智谱、 MINIMAX 均跌超 6% 。债市⽅⾯，国债期货
全线反弹⾛⾼。商品⽅⾯，国内商品期货涨跌互现。

### Page 4

银⾏逆势新⾼：四⼤⾏ A+H 总市值历史⾸次突破 10 万亿
⼯商银⾏涨 2.57% （ A 股总市值 2.94 万亿元、居 A 股市值第⼀）、建设银⾏涨 2.29% （ 2.35 万亿
元、居 A 股市值第⼆），双双续创历史新⾼；农业银⾏、中国银⾏、交通银⾏、成都银⾏盘中集
体创历史新⾼。
城商⾏弹性更⼤，⻘岛银⾏涨 8.02% 报 6.33 元领涨，成都银⾏涨 6.08% ，⻬鲁银⾏涨 4.49% ，瑞
丰银⾏、南京银⾏、重庆银⾏等跟涨。

### Page 5

标志性事件是， " ⼯农中建 " 四⼤⾏ A+H 股总市值历史⾸次突破 10 万亿元，其中⼯⾏ 2.94 万亿、建
⾏ 2.35 万亿、农⾏ 2.58 万亿、中⾏ 2.15 万亿。
这⼀轮银⾏⾏情由多重逻辑共同⽀撑：
其⼀，净息差拐点确认。国家⾦融监督管理总局数据显⽰， 2026 年⼆季度商业银⾏净息差
1.41% ，环⽐回升 1 个基点，这是⾃ 2022 年以来商业银⾏净息差⾸次实现单季度环⽐增⻓，银⾏
的估值锚正从息差单边下⾏切换到可持续的分红回报。
其⼆，中期分红密集落地。截⾄⽬前已有 21 家 A 股上市银⾏披露 2026 年中期分红⽅案，合计拟
派息超 2600 亿元，涵盖国有六⼤⾏、 4 家股份⾏、 6 家城商⾏和 5 家农商⾏；其中六⼤⾏合计拟派
现约 2209.89 亿元、占⽐超⼋成，六⼤⾏分红⽐例由 30% 提升⾄ 31% 。
其三，外部评级与资⾦双重加持。惠誉评级 10 ⽉ 27 ⽇上调六家中资国有银⾏的⽣存⼒评级，并
将中资银⾏经营环境评分⾃ "bbb-/ 稳定 " 调整⾄ "bbb/ 稳定 " ；同时被动资⾦持续流⼊银⾏板块，据

测算截⾄ 10 ⽉ 23 ⽇全市场被动型指数 ETF 近 1 ⽉净流⼊ 157.58 亿元，⽀撑板块估值修复。
西部证券称，银⾏股这段时间既有绝对收益、⼜能跑出相对收益，⼀⽅⾯是宏观弱周期中上市
银⾏中报业绩仍保持稳健，绩优⾏带动板块估值中枢阶段性上修；另⼀⽅⾯是三季度以来科技
估值 " 挤泡沫 " 、成交缩量下， " 低估 + ⾼股息 + 市值体量⼤ " 的银⾏板块成为阶段承接调仓资⾦
的 " 蓄⽔池 " 。
华福证券认为，当前银⾏股估值相较 2025 年并没有明显⾼估， PB 估值明显低于上年⾼点、股息
率较利率有相对⽐价优势；从规律上看，银⾏股四季度⼀般都有超额⾏情 —— 公募基⾦从⾼波
动成⻓向低波动蓝筹切换、险资提前布局开⻔红对⾼股息资产有天然需求、银⾏盈利确定性较
强。

### Page 6

商业航天逆势涨停潮：上海卫星互联⽹⾏动⽅案落地催化
中国卫星震荡冲⾼、强势封住涨停；上海沪⼯、航天晨光、西部材料等多股涨停；邵阳液压直
线拉升封住 20% 涨停；上海港湾涨停，天⼒复合、⻜沃科技、铖昌科技等涨超 10% 。
催化来⾃政策、产业、海外三端共振：
政策端 —— 上海给出明确产业⽬标。 10 ⽉ 26 ⽇，上海市经济信息化委等六部⻔印发《上海市卫
星互联⽹产业创新发展⾏动⽅案（ 2027—2030 年）》，明确到 2030 年上海卫星互联⽹核⼼产业规
模突破 800 亿元、在轨卫星总数超 3000 颗、培育优质企业超 100 家、打造 "100+ 标杆⽰范应⽤ " ，
建成具有全球影响⼒的卫星互联⽹产业创新⾼地。
产业端 —— ⼤会成果密集落地。 10 ⽉ 27 ⽇⾄ 29 ⽇ 2026 卫星互联⽹产业⽣态⼤会在上海松江举
办，上海正式启动筹建卫星互联⽹产业基⾦（由市区两级投资平台联合社会资本共同发起），并
披露 " 星枢计划 " 天基算⼒部署路线。
海外端 —— 卫星直连⼿机加速。美国联邦通信委员会（ FCC ）本⽉正式批准 SpaceX 部署多达

1.5 万颗直连⼿机（ D2D ）卫星，为全球卫星互联⽹商业化注⼊催化剂。

增⻓ 16.24% ，净利润 5036.34 万元、同⽐增⻓ 11.17% ；前三季度累计营收 39.56 亿元、同⽐增⻓
27.52% ，归⺟净利润 8492.91 万元、同⽐⼤增 473.40% 。

基础设施投资的重要增量；短期政策⽀持重点仍将围绕关键技术突破和产业基础设施建设展
开，⽕箭回收复⽤是其中核⼼环节；中⻓期政策重⼼将逐步转向促进卫星应⽤商业化，推动低
轨卫星由基础设施投资转变为商业运营服务。

10% ，市场担忧云⼤⼚资本开⽀节奏放缓，情绪直接传导⾄ A 股 CPO/ 光模块产业链。

Document 321: Fed holds rates steady despite inflation being too high

Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 5

### Page 1

Fed Chair Kevin Warsh acknowledged that progress is being
made, but inﬂation remains too high and a December FOMC
rate hike remains our call

James Knightley, Padhraic Garvey, CFA and Chris Turner
Federal Reserve Chair Warsh announced that interest rates remain unchanged
10-2 vote to keep policy unchanged
The Federal Reserve voted 10-2 in favour of leaving the Fed funds
target rate range at 3.75%-4%. Only 2bp of a potential 25bp hike
was priced ahead of the decision and only 6 of 97 organisations
polled by Bloomberg predicted a hike, so this was no surprise and
market reaction has been limited.
Senior Fed oﬃcials, including Vice Chairs Philip Jeﬀerson and
Michelle Bowman and Governor Chris Waller, suggested ahead of
the decision that they were prepared to move cautiously, seeing
little need for back-to-back rate hikes after September's policy
change. Nonetheless, Beth Hammack and Lisa Cook both felt that
the current situation warranted additional action. Cook’s vote for a

hike was a surprise given the general sense that she was a centrist,
and it will do nothing to improve her fraught relationship with
President Trump. Instead, we had suspected it would be one or both
of Neel Kashkari and Lorrie Logan (who both also dissented in July)
that would join Hammack in voting in favour of a further rate hike.
There aren’t many changes in the accompanying press release,
with the economy described as “expanding at a strong pace” and

“inﬂation remains elevated”. There was no pre-commitment to
further action, with it being the data that “will help the Committee
determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return
of inﬂation to the Committee's 2 percent goal."
At the press conference, Kevin Warsh oﬀered no real guidance other
than to say that inﬂation remains too high, but the overwhelming
consensus in the FOMC is that the “medicine” is starting to work.
Moreover, the Fed will deliver price stability, “no ifs, ands or buts." He

did state that from next September one FOMC meeting won’t have
a rate vote, leaving it to focus exclusively on monetary policy
strategy. From 2028, this will become two meetings, leaving only
six meetings at which interest rate decisions will be made.
US inﬂation and unemployment

December rate hike remains the most likely

### Page 3

There is the valid question that, given a majority of FOMC members
project a rate hike before the end of the year, why didn’t they take
that decision today? Kevin Warsh’s response was that he himself
didn’t make a prediction and, in any case, they removed some
policy accommodation in September. Inﬂation is “going the right
way” and he is encouraged by the progress made.
We will have two jobs reports and one inﬂation print ahead of the
December decision. The November CPI will be released the day after

the Fed meeting, and oﬃcials will almost certainly get to see it. As
such, they will have more information than the market does,
meaning a surprise can’t be completely ruled out. For now, a
December hike looks a solid bet, which would leave the Fed funds
target rate range at 4.00%-4.25%.
The sharp move higher in Treasury yields and the ﬁrmer dollar are
tightening ﬁnancial conditions to a degree, but our forecast path
for the Fed assumes that in the couple of months following the

midterm elections we see an agreement that improves the ﬂow of
energy from the Persian Gulf, but signiﬁcantly, allows for a sharp
reduction in shipping and insurance costs. It's the latter point that is
crucial to delivering lower pump prices at a time when reﬁneries are
working at capacity.
If correct, this should meaningfully ease inﬂation pressures, and
with US wage growth (the biggest cost input for corporate America)
looking very benign at just 3% and the largest component of the

inﬂation basket – housing at 35% by weight – set to experience
more disinﬂation, we believe overall US inﬂation can fall back to 2%
in the second half of 2027.
Hence, we subsequently anticipate a prolonged pause through to
December 2027, when we have that December 2026 hike being
reversed. This is in line with the current consensus forecast, while
the Fed’s own projections (its central forecast) see one hike this year
with rates left unchanged throughout 2027.

The lean hawkish is enough to validate the
break back above 5% for the 10yr

### Page 4

Hardly a word in the short statement that is not leaning hawkish.
But no hike. Prior to the FOMC meeting, it was remarkable how the
10yr yield had been clinging to the 4.99% level. It was like a
comfort level; technically below 5%, but not too far below in case it
needs to turn tail and shoot back up again. It seemed that the US
10yr was waiting to tick oﬀ this outcome before deviating too
much from 5%. Well, it’s now had the outcome. The initial reaction

was up. But the reaction to Chair Warsh’s words at the press
conference was to revert down to 5%, and brieﬂy below. This can be
rationalised by Warsh’s comforting words on inﬂation expectations,
which he deemed to have been tamed somewhat.
That all being said, our interpretation is that this Fed still has a job
to do if the type of price stability that Chair Warsh has described is
to be achieved. He has described price stability as strictly 2%, and

he seems to be serious about hitting that. This is good from a
protective sense for the back end. At the same time, it’s tough to
view the current set-up of still elevated printed inﬂation and a Fed
on hold as all that protective. Also, inﬂation expectations in fact
rose post the Fed decision and subsequent Warsh commentary. We
glean from this enough to validate the move back above 5% on the
10yr. We maintain our view that we’re on a journey back up in the

direction of 5.25%.
The implementation note was broadly unchanged in terms of
action. The various implementation rates are unchanged, as are the
implementation mechanisms. And the Fed will continue to reinvest
principal payments from its MBS securities into Treasury bills. We’re
likely still some way from the Fed spelling out a plan to shrink its
balance sheet, which we know remains a policy objective for Chair
Warsh. We expect recommendations from the various committees

to surface closer to the turn of the year. Meanwhile, liquidity
conditions are stable, with repo behaving far better than it did a
year ago.
Dollar softens a touch
Perhaps it was a belated acknowledgement that there would not be
forward guidance on a December rate hike, but the lack of clarity

The dollar reaction – a slight softening – probably owed to

recent months, solid US activity and French budget woes have
largely seen one-way traﬃc in the dollar since early September.
However, the overall tone of the press conference, strong and
broadening US growth and above-target inﬂation, suggests the

dollar does not need to correct much at all at this stage.
Up next will be the ECB meeting on Thursday and the Bank of
Japan meeting on Friday. Barring major hawkish surprises at either
of those meetings, we would expect the dollar to continue to
consolidate towards the top of recent ranges.

Document 381: Rates Spark_ Hikes coming, but bond yields choosing to fall

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rates Spark_ Hikes coming, but bond yields choosing to fall.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 3

### Page 1

US Treasuries are in a mood to test the downside for yields
after all. Still not sold on following through on it. But
monitoring. The ECB meeting did not change our rates outlook
and conﬁrms a December hike is likely. We brace for more
volatility as Friday's CPI and GDP readings from the eurozone
are important ingredients in markets' rate hike expectations

Padhraic Garvey, CFA, Benjamin Schroeder and Michiel Tukker
As with the Federal Reserve, we look for a December rate hike by the ECB as
upside inﬂation risks persist
US Treasuries looking through the rate hike
narrative for December
The US 10yr yield made a decisive gap away from 5%, but to the
downside. This was not a fundamentally driven move, in the sense
that Thursday's PCE inﬂation data continued to point to an
ongoing signiﬁcant deviation from where Chair Warsh would like to
see it at (he reiterated in the aftermath of the FOMC meeting that
he wants to see 2% on core, but it rose to 3.1% for September). The

### Page 2

income, spending and jobless claims data were also in line, and at
the ﬁrm end of the range.
Instead, Treasuries are willing to remain quite responsive to the
vagaries of the sentiment on AI valuations and equity market
wobbles. Nothing terribly sinister went down here, but the ongoing
mood music has just turned that bit sour. Meanwhile, the 7yr
auction was ﬁne, with no tail, and met decent demand.
What we can say is the move was more a back-end thing than a

front-end one. Some chunky ﬂows did go through on the buy side.
We can't ignore this move, as it's contrary to our preferred morph
back in the direction of higher yields. But there is not enough in it to
cause us to change our tune.
ECB close to a next hike
Although debating it, the European Central Bank (ECB) refrained
from a rate hike on Thursday. The tightening of ﬁnancial conditions
amid rising bond market yields tipped the balance, as they have

done the work for the ECB.
The signal that a December hike was in the oﬃng was pretty clear.
Unless we get any positive developments in the Middle East, or
adverse developments in French bond markets, December looks like
a done deal. And markets have been treating it that way already,
with more than 20bp of tightening discounted.
The market takeaway from Thursday's events was still a less
hawkish one. A Reuters report about ECB staﬀ estimating that

higher bond yields could cut cumulative growth by 100bp and
inﬂation by 90bp in 2027-2028 relative to the September projection
adds to the notion that the December hike could be the ECB’s last
of the cycle.
While this is also our baseline scenario, more needs to fall into
place for markets to lean more in that direction. Easing energy
prices and no evidence of second-round eﬀects would certainly
help, but especially on the latter, greater certainty needs to be

established over months of tracking the data.

Currently, the market still sees the upside inﬂation risks ﬂagged by

the next 12 months has dropped towards 65bp, this was mainly
driven by French-related risk-oﬀ momentum and, of late, also
growing AI jitters. As arguments for the ECB to change its tack, the
spread widening thus far will likely not be suﬃcient, meaning the
market will remain exposed to bearish risks.
Friday’s events and market views
A data-intense day for the eurozone, including CPI numbers

from France, Spain and Germany. In addition, we also have
eurozone GDP estimates for the third quarter. Rates have
been pushed higher by second-round inﬂation fears and still
robust growth, so all these releases will feed directly into the
euro curve. Also watch for comments from ECB speakers
reﬂecting on Thursday's meeting and reacting to the many
data points.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

People Bank of China (October 20)

We expect the PBOC to keep the 1Y LPR 
unchanged next week . Industrial 
production is expected to edge up from 
5.2% yoy in August to 5.3% in September, 
supported by firm external demand. 
Exports stayed strong, rising 36.3% yoy 
in September, driven by the regional AI -
electronics upcycle.

Domestic momentum, however, remains 
uneven. Retail sales growth is expected 
to moderate to 0.3% yoy  in September. 
Household sentiment is weak amid 
uncertain job prospects, slower income 
growth and elevated precautionary 
savings, while subdued property prices 
continue to weigh on household wealth 
and consumption. Investment also stays 
weak, with fixed as set investment 
expected to contract further by 7.5% yoy 
ytd in September. Corporate investment 
is cautious amid the anti -involution 
campaign and geopolitical tensions, 
while the property sector continues to be 
a major drag.

Policymakers are likely to maintain a 
measured policy stance, relying on 
targeted fiscal support to sustain growth 
rather than further rate cuts.

Bank Indonesia and Bangko Sentral ng 
Pilipinas (Oct 21 and 22).

Bank Indonesia is expected to extend its 
pause, as they lean on non-rate measures, 
including intervention and hedging 
facilities to help the currency.  On Friday, 
MSCI signaled a  potential shift in 
Indonesia’s status to a standalone 
status, rather than a reclassification to 
frontier market , after consultations are 
complete in mid -2027. Any ensuing risk -
off move in the currency , would likely 
prompt greater central bank vigilance 
and intervention.

Our base case for the benchmark rate to 
be held unchanged this quarter , backed 
by a lower conviction around back -to-
back US rate hikes in Oct and relative 
rupiah stability. Separately, the 
government announced additional 
support for low-income households in 4Q 
by way of in -kind assistance and wage 
subsidies. This year’s fiscal deficit i s 
likely to close in at -2.85% of GDP, close 
to our forecast at -2.9%, but few 
uncertainties facing the 2027 math (note).

We expect a 25bp hike from the BSP to 
5.25% in October , after three rate 
increases earlier in the year. Rising by the 
highest pace in Asia, Philippines inflation 
surged to 7.2% y/y in September from 6.1% 
in August, matching this year's peak and 
approaching a three -year high.  Inflation 
risks remain tilted to the upside, driven by 
further public transport fare adjustments, 
elevated oil prices, recent wage hikes 
and peso depreciation. Fiscal space is, 
meanwhile, limited to support 
households, via subsidies and/ instate 
price caps. Governor Remolona also 
signalled that the BSP remains ready to 
act on rates to contain inflation. With 
inflationary pressures likely to be 
persistent, the likelihood of further hikes 
in 4Q26 rises.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Bank of Korea (October 22)

The Bank of Korea is expected to hold its 
policy rate at 3.00%, following back -to-
back rate hikes in July and August. AI -
driven growth momentum remains strong, 
as reflected in September export growth 
of around 80% YoY. However, the 
urgency for further tightening has eased, 
with CPI inflation stabilising at 2.9% YoY 
in September. The recent KOSPI 
correction and continued KRW strength 
also reduce the need for another near -
term hike. We maintain our call for a final 
25bp rate hike in November, bringing the 
policy rate to 3.25% by year-end.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Japan

September trade and CPI data are due 
shortly. Export growth is expected to 
remain solid at 24% YoY, supported by 
continued strength in AI -related 
semiconductor exports. CPI inflation is 
projected to rise notably to 2.6% YoY, 
driven by the phase -out of publ ic utility 
subsidies, higher global energy prices, 
and persistent inflationary pressures 
from a weak yen. The BOJ is likely to 
increasingly acknowledge that its 2% 
inflation target is within reach. However, 
it is expected to keep rates unchanged 
at 1.25% at its October 29–30 meeting to 
assess the impact of the latest rate hike 
in September.

Taiwan

September export orders and industrial 
production data are due shortly. Export 
orders are expected to sustain 60% YoY 
growth, while industrial production is 
projected to expand by 20% YoY. The 
global AI boom faces growing headwinds 
from hyperscalers’ cash flow pressures, 
central bank rate hikes, and higher bond 
yields. However, our high -frequency 
indicators across AI demand (token 
expenditure), investment (hyperscaler  
capex and data centre construction 
spending), and supply (semiconductor 
sales and exports) show no clear signs of 
a significant slowdown so far.

Malaysia

Malaysia’s external demand likely 
remained strong in September 2026, 
while inflation edged up slightly. We 
expect export growth to sustain its 
robust expansion at 45% yoy , with 
momentum anchored by strong demand 
for electrical and electronics goods. Oil 
& gas shipments likely remained buoyant. 
However, high base effects prevented a 
faster pace of expansion.

We expect headline inflation to rise 
modestly to 2.1% yoy in September 2026. 
The renewed increase in global oil prices 
likely contributed to higher increases in 
domestic energy costs, although fuel 
subsidies continued to limit the cost 
pass-through and keep underlying 
inflationary pressures contained. Food 
inflation likely also firmed, with adverse 
weather conditions associated with El 
Nino posing upside risks.

Singapore

We expect Singapore’s core and 
headline inflation to edge up slightly to 
2.3% yoy and 2.4% yoy , respectively, in 
September 2026, following modest 
increases to 2.2% yoy and 2.3% yoy in 
August. Inflationary pressures likely 
reflected firm private transport price 
increases amid higher petrol prices 
despite slower car price growth , the 
ongoing pass -through of higher global 
energy prices to fuel -exposed services 
items, positive contribution from 
elevated electricity & gas prices, firmer 
food inflation, and a slight increase in 
still-muted accommodation inflation. 
Firm, albeit not acc elerating, inflation in 
September 2026, which may be close to 
its peak in the Monetary Authority of 
Singapore’s assessment, likely validated 
its decision to maintain SGD NEER policy 
settings at the October 2026 review, 
following two proactive tightening 
moves in April and July.

Hong Kong

CPI is projected to edge up from 1.7% yoy 
in August to 1.9% in September, 
supported by improving consumption 
sentiment and higher global energy 
prices. Mainland visitor arrivals 
increased by 29.0% yoy in September on 
a daily average basis, providing furthe r 
support to retail spending and 
consumer-related services. Meanwhile, 
renewed conflict in the Middle East 
during the month has pushed up global 
energy prices, adding to upward 
pressure on utility and transport costs.

^New 
Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 27) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

The Foundation and Foundation+ models are allocations that you can use as the starting point for building a diversified 
investment portfolio. The Foundation model showcases a set of allocations focusing on traditional asset classes that are 
accessible to most investors, while the Found ation+ model includes allocations to private assets that may be accessible to 
investors in some jurisdictions, but not others.

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027 , as AI-led expansion continues.  The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4 %, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2. 3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026 , but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risk s 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures ( PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees c ore PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2. 4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates  at 4.0% , after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8 
ECB to hike once more  in December, then pause . The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5%  y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer -term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy , and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year . Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets , which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non -manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD  133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though , with  technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key  rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first -home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local -government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a  quarterly pace  until Q2 2027.  Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4% , and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five -year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3%  in Q1 2027 . Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle  in H2 2027. Two government -appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an  Underweight view on Developed Market ( DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data . With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps  hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3 -7-year part of the  US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We remain Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, 
government bond yields have retraced meaningfully after a 
sharp bear steepening through September and early October. 
We expect real yield s to stay elevated over the next six 
months as AI capex and productivity growth continue to drive 
above-trend economic growth. We expect two more 25bps 
rate hikes by June 2027.  
Over the next few months, we expect US government bond 
yields to remain rangebound. The near-term path hinges on 
five factors: 1) the 3 November US mid-term election, where 
a ‘Democratic sweep’ would likely be bond -supportive by 
constraining US President Trump’s fiscal spending agenda , 
while a ‘Republican sweep’ would likely weigh on bonds as 
markets price in further fiscal expansion and a higher term 
premium; 2) Fed communication at upcoming policy meetings; 
3) the US Treasury’s quarterly refunding announcement on 4 
November, where a ny signal of reduced long -end issuance  
would likely compress long-end yields; 4) softening auction 
demand and repatriation by foreign investors, which add 
uncertainty around demand for US government bonds; and 5) 
the f luid geopolitical situation in the Middle East , which is 
keeping oil prices elevated, with a risk of further upside. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect moderating oil prices and the 
fading inflationary impact of tariffs to give the Fed room to 
consider rate cuts. The US 30-year yield, however, is likely to 
stay elevated as fiscal deficit remains substantial next year.  
We expect a final ECB hike to 2.75% by December 2026, then 
a pause in H1 2027, followed by a 25bps cut in Q3 2027. In 
Japan, we expect quarterly pace of BoJ hikes, with one hike 
that takes the policy rate to 1.50% by December 2026 and two 
more in H1 2027, reaching 2.00% before pausing at mid-year.

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10 
DM corporates – Core holding 
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across DM credit. Credit spreads have widened from 
their lows but remain close to historical tights, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight-spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion,  
especially in the IG space, where heavy AI-related issuance 
is weighing on the long end of the curve. AI -driven supply is 
surpassing USD 2 50bn in 2026 and mak ing up a growing 
share of US IG gross issuance, but there are growing signs 
that hyperscalers are turning to private markets for fundin g, 
which should help ease public bond market supply pressures.  
We remain selective at the long end  of the yield curve and  
favour a duration of  3-7 years, where risk -adjusted carry 
remains most durable. Issuance-driven concessions in 
sectors with strong capex cycles (notably technology, capital 
goods and basic industries) present opportunities for investors 
who can stomach near-term volatility. 
Fig. 12 DM IG spreads have widened from their lows, 
but remain near historically tight levels 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
DM HY spreads  have widened to roughly a six-month high. 
Persistent doubts about whether hyperscalers can generate 
adequate returns on massive AI capex investments triggered 
a broad risk asset sell-off. The CCC tier has become the focal 
point of genuine credit concern, with yields hovering near the 
high-15% range and spreads near a four-year high. Market 
focus has shifted to the funding pressures facing AI-related 
neocloud companies. We view this  as idiosyncratic for now 
and see a limited impact on the broader HY universe.  The 
underlying credit picture is mixed. Leverage is elevated and 
interest coverage is thin, but the broad market has not yet 
shown signs of systemic stress. The US HY d efault rate  
remains below trend in 2026. 
HY offers an attractive income proposition, with all-in yields of 
over 8%. Its shorter-duration profile, combined with its lower 
sensitivity to changes in the term premium and rate volatility, 
make it an attractive sleeve in the current phase of the cycle.  
We remain attentive to evolving credit rating migration trends 
and sector-specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades 
slow and new issuance clusters around key themes. However, 
healthy issuer fu ndamentals and stable credit metrics  
continue to support it as a Core holding allocation. 
Fig. 13 DM HY spreads are off the tights 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight 
USD/Core holding for LCY 
We are Overweight EM USD government bonds and retain 
EM LCY government bonds as a Core holding, reflecting a 
balanced perspective across both regional performance and 
currency dynamics. In EM USD sovereign debt, the recent 
spread widening offered an attractive entry point to gain 
exposure to areas benefiting from resilient global growth and 
stable external conditions. Technicals remain favourable, with 
supply being front-loaded and investor positioning remaining 
relatively light compared with historical levels. 
Many EM central banks having tightened earlier and more 
aggressively post -pandemic, preserving credibility and 
maintaining positive real rates. External balances have 
improved, reserve buffers have increased and greater 
reliance on domestic funding reduces external vulnerability. 
Lula’s surprise win in Brazil’s presidential election raises 
political risk. The country’s fiscal trajectory under the incoming 
administration is a key risk to monitor. Fiscal slippage could  
trigger a broader repricing of LATAM risk. In Asia, higher-rated 
issuers continue to offer a valuable buffer during bouts of 
global rate volatility, while select frontier exposures in EMEA 
remain more sensitive to liquidity shifts and changes in risk 
appetite. Index composition also shapes resilience: the EM 
USD benchmark is less exposed to oil price shocks , as 
countries that are not oil importers account for a larger share 
of the benchmark than in  the LCY benchmark. We expect 
returns to be primarily driven by carry rather than further 
spread compression.

### Page 11

Global Market Outlook 11 
Fig. 14 EM USD government bonds offer a yield pick-up 
of nearly 3ppt over DM peers 
Bloomberg EM USD Government Bond Index, yield to worst

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM LCY debt faces additional headwinds, especially as 
elevated US real yields and an appreciating USD dampen 
demand from foreign investors and reduce the comparative 
attractiveness of broad -based duration strategies. However, 
real yields remain comparatively supportive in several 
markets, and current valuations signal selective opportunities, 
especially given the restrictive monetary policy stances 
prevalent across many EM economies. Accordingly, EM LCY 
bonds retain their Core holding status, with portfolio emphasis 
placed on country selection and measured duration exposure, 
reflecting the differentiated currency dynamics at play. 
Key risks to both  the USD and LCY segments include a 
potential escalation of the  Middle East conflict , higher oil 
prices, renewed hawkishness from the Fed that could support 
further USD strength and weather-related disruptions, such as 
the effects of El Niño . Climate risks could prolong inflation 
pressures and hinder disinflation in vulnerable EM regions. 
Asia USD bonds – Core holding 
We retain a Neutral allocation to Asia USD bonds, an asset 
class that continues to reward investors with attractive 
nominal yields, favourable supply -demand technicals and 
robust credit fundamentals. Healthy corporate cash flows, low 
leverage and a high share of sovereign-linked issuers lend the 
segment a defensive quality relative to broader credit. 
The resilience has been evident year to date (YTD). Asia USD 
bonds have outperformed  both DM government and DM IG 
corporate bonds  on a total -return basis. We expect this 
relative strength to persist. The combination of carry and 
credit quality (over 60% of issues are sovereign or sovereign-
related) anchors our conviction in the asset class, even as we 
maintain a Neutral stance.

Bond opportunistic views 
Bullish US HY bonds 
We are opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. We expect 
HY corporate earnings and cash flows to remain solid and 
leverage to remain stable. US HY bonds’  shorter-duration 
profile offers lower sensitivity to interest-rate volatility, which 
is an attractive characteristic in an environment of elevated 
term premia. 
Bullish US inflation-protected bonds (TIPS) 
Real yields remain elevated versus historical standards , 
driven by rate hike expectations. Inflation concerns persist as 
energy prices remain subject to upside risks. We believe TIPS 
offer protection against near-term inflation uncertainty.   
Bullish AUD corporate bonds 
The threshold for further RBA hikes has risen, as higher 
energy prices weigh on the growth outlook and complicate the 
policy trade-off. The transmission channel matters too, where 
higher long -end yields could feed through to the property 
market, tightening financial conditions without the RBA having 
to act, casting further doubt on the case for additional hikes. 
Technicals have improved slightly as higher yields slow new 
supply. Supply remains a tail risk, though. The AUD bond

market is much smaller than the USD market, especially at the 
long end of the curve, where demand for duration is thinner 
and the term premium is most sensitive to supply. 
Bullish AAA-rated CLOs 
Concerns about spillovers from p rivate credit are mounting 
amid deteriorating covenant protections in parts of the 
leveraged credit market. However, we believe AAA-rated 
CLOs backed by solid asset portfolios and substantial credit 
enhancements underneath senior tranches should help 
dampen credit risk and volatility. We see a compelling case 
for floating-rate and shorter -duration alternatives as portfolio 
diversifiers amid elevated term premia.  
Close utilities corporate hybrids 
AI capex is set to keep rising, but funding it has become 
harder. Tighter financial conditions now coincide with growing 
competition for capital. Utilities' credit fundamentals remain 
strong. Even so, we close our utilities corporate hybrids idea 
to lock in a gain of 0.5% as a risk-management step.

### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12

Our view

We downgrade global equities from Overweight to a Core allocation as we expect higher volatility in the near term. We 
expect this volatility to arise as equity market leadership broadens during the transition to a mid-cycle bull market. Equity returns 
will likely moderate from the 15-20% annualised range of the last few years but remain positive. We remain Overweight US and 
AxJ equities, which are expected to deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 among the major regions. 
Within AxJ, we remain Overweight Taiwan, given structural AI-driven growth, and upgrade India to Overweight, for diversified 
non-AI growth exposure. South Korea is a Core holding, with attractive valuations but extreme volatility . China is also a Core 
holding, with attractive valuations but lacking a positive catalyst. We are Underweight ASEAN, which has a weaker growth profile. 
We maintain Japan as a Core holding as nominal growth supports broad-based earnings revisions. We also maintain a Core 
allocation to Europe ex-UK, which is enjoying an earnings recovery. We remain Underweight UK equities, given the market’s 
limited exposure to growth sectors.

### Page 13

Global Market Outlook 13

Shifting to a mid-cycle bull market  
The last few years have seen strong annual returns of 15-20% 
for global equities , consistent with the early cycle of a bull 
market, driven in large part by a rapid AI buildout. As the AI 
infrastructure buildout is increasingly debt funded  – we 
estimate 40% of AI capex in 2027 would be debt -financed – 
the market is increasingly concerned about the quality of this 
growth and execution risks . W e remain positive on the  
structural AI theme, but we expect investor focus to shift from 
rapid buildout to monetisation . During this transition, we 
expect market leadership to broaden  beyond technology. 
We believe this shift will also be accompanied by higher near-
term volatility and a moderation in global equity returns , 
consistent with a transition to a mid-cycle bull market.  
Fig. 16 Projected 16% earnings growth for global 
equities in 2027 provides a strong tailwind for positive 
equity returns  
2026 and 2027 consensus earnings growth estimates for 
MSCI AC World sectors

Source: FactSet, Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  
US equities – Overweight  
US equity valuations have declined this year, with the 12-
month forward P/E falling to 19x from 22x at the start of the 
year. This reflects higher rates, a valuation headwind, a nd 
worries about the quality of growth, which is increasingly debt-
funded. As market leadership broadens and the US mid-term 
election nears, we expect higher near-term volatility in the US 
market. Nonetheless, US equities have broad-based strength, 
with the ongoing Q3 earnings season point ing to a robust 
economy. In a world of rising geopolitical risks, the US is also 
relatively insulated by its domestically driven growth. 
AxJ equities – Overweight 
AxJ has the strongest earnings growth  among major 
markets, as its technology sector remains a key beneficiary of 
the AI capex cycle. AxJ’s P/E valuations have also dropped to 
attractive trough levels, the lowest among major regions. 
Within AxJ, we are Overweight Taiwan, which benefits from 
clear visibility on AI -driven growth and dominance in 
manufacturing advanced AI chips. We also upgrade India to  
Overweight due to its resilient domestically driven earnings, 
providing diversification beyond AI-related growth. India offers 
the highest exposure to defensive sectors (healthcare, 
consumer staples and utilities) within AxJ.  Both Taiwan and 
India offer attractive returns on equity.  
South Korea equities remain a Core holding. We are positive 
on long-term agreements that improve earnings visibility for 
memory chipmakers. Valuations are attractive  (the lowest in 
AxJ), with supportive corporate governance reforms . Still, 
extreme volatility remains the key overhang. China remains 
a Core holding , given inexpensive valuations , but re-rating 
potential appears delayed amid AI moneti sation struggles, 
lingering regulatory risks and weak domestic demand. We 
remain Underweight ASEAN , as Indonesia’s downgrade 
warning, oil shocks and a well-supported USD could weigh on 
the region, although Singapore offers defensive growth. 
Fig. 17 India offers highest AxJ exposure to defensive 
sectors (healthcare, consumer staples and utilities) 
Sector weights for MSCI equity indices

Source: FactSet, Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
Japan equities – Core allocation 
Rising nominal growth is supporting solid broad-based 
earnings revisions , with rising rates supportive of bank 
earnings. Corporate governance reforms also continue to 
boost shareholder value . The fiscal support for strategic 
industries is pro-growth, offsetting energy shocks.  
Europe ex-UK equities – Core allocation  
Europe ex-UK is seeing an earnings recovery after three 
stagnant years, and we expect to see a continuation of this 
narrative with Q3 results. Rising rates are supportive of banks’ 
earnings and fiscal expansion is benefiting domestic demand. 
The fiscal situation in France has no easy solution, but we do 
not expect a contagion widening in government bond spreads, 
as the ECB has tools in place to limit contagion.  
UK equities – Underweight  
The UK lacks exposure to growth sectors, with relatively 
muted earnings growth. We expect this to weigh on the UK, 
making it lag the performance of other markets over the next 
12 months, although UK equities tend to hold up better during 
bouts of volatility.

Global Market Outlook 14

Add MSCI Switzerland and US Healthcare, 
remove Global Power & Electrification, MSCI 
Taiwan, Global Semis and US Comm Services 
We initiate an opportunistic Idea on MSCI Switzerland on 29 
October 2026, having initiated one on US Healthcare on 12 
October 2026. Healthcare and Consumer Staples account for 
over 50% of the MSCI Switzerland  Index’s weight, providing 
exposure to more resilient demand. It also has a sizeable 
financial sector supported by steady wealth management 
growth and well -capitalised insurers , which benefit from 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We closed our opportunistic ideas in Global 
Semiconductors (9 Sep tember 2026 to 2 Oct ober 2026: 
+8.1%), MSCI Taiwan  (9 Jul y 2026 to 2 Oct ober 2026: 
+10.0%), US Communication Services (18 June 2026 to 12 
October 2026: +1.9%), and Global Power & Electrification  
(30 Jul y 2026 to 29 Oct ober 2026: -6.5%). This reflects a 
tactical reduction in exposure to tech -related sectors amid 
higher expected  near-term volatility, rather than a 
deterioration in the fundamental outlook. Over the longer term, 
we remain constructive on these structural themes and may 
revisit these ideas at more attractive entry points.
